## STRATEGIES AND HARD RULES
[shr-00001] Make sure to end code blocks with ``` followed by a newline(\n).
[shr-00005] Always look at API specifications (using apis.api_docs.show_api_doc) before calling an API.
[shr-00006] Write small chunks of code and only one chunk of code in every step. Make sure everything is working correctly before making any irreversible change.

[shr-00015] API-doc inspection has no exceptions: before the first call to any API, including supervisor APIs such as show_account_passwords and complete_task, call apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='supervisor', api_name=...). Do not assume simple-looking account or completion APIs are self-explanatory.
[shr-00030] Do not infer current date from access-token/JWT exp claims or decode tokens; if no authoritative current-date API is available, use the latest-expiring payment-card heuristic. Do not use disallowed modules such as base64 to attempt token decoding.
[shr-00038] Before applying the latest-expiry payment-card heuristic, explicitly search relevant app/API descriptions for an authoritative current-date/time API; if none exists, record that no current-date API is available and then choose max((expiry_year, expiry_month)). Do not infer current date from JWT/access-token exp.
[shr-00039] Maintain an inspected_apis set for API-doc compliance: before the first call to any API, call apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name=..., api_name=...) and add the tuple to inspected_apis; assert ('supervisor', 'complete_task') in inspected_apis before calling apis.supervisor.complete_task(). This prevents skipping documentation for final/simple APIs.
[shr-00043] When applying the latest-expiry payment-card heuristic, search API descriptions for an authoritative current-date/time API across all relevant apps (amazon, supervisor, phone), not just the purchasing app; record the apps searched and that no date API was found before selecting max((expiry_year, expiry_month)).
[shr-00051] Before executing any code block, verify it is syntactically complete: no dangling for/if/while/def, balanced parentheses/brackets, and no truncated statements. If the block is long, split it into smaller chunks (login, product selection, evidence collection, parsing, completion) instead of emitting one fragile monolith.
[shr-00070] Inspect API docs for every API referenced in any conditional branch before executing the code block, even if current data makes the branch false; e.g., add_gift_wrapping_to_product and apply_promo_code_to_cart must be inspected before cart-restoration code that conditionally calls them.
[shr-00076] Never convert printed Amazon search matches into literal product_ids in later code. In the same block that filters exact normalized matches, assert len(matches) == 1 and assign pid = matches[0]['product_id']; build requested_items from those pid variables. If a subsequent block does not have the variable, re-derive it programmatically from search/show_product rather than copying an ID from output.
[shr-00080] For 'do whatever is cheaper' purchase tasks, compare complete cart totals, not isolated fees: build each candidate option in the cart, store option_a_total = show_cart(...).get('total_cost') and option_b_total = show_cart(...).get('total_cost') in variables, assert the selected option is cheaper (or use a deterministic tie-breaker if equal), then order only the selected option. Every product_id used in an option, including auxiliary items such as wrapping paper, must come from a programmatic candidate-selection variable, not printed output.
[shr-00085] When a task requires comparing multiple cart configurations (e.g., Amazon gift wrap vs. separate wrapping paper), save each option's total and cart state, then clear the cart and rebuild only the chosen option before place_order. Assert the current cart's total_cost equals chosen_total and its product/gift-wrap state matches the chosen option; never order the last-built option by accident.
[shr-00088] Enforce inspected_apis universally: initialize inspected_apis = set(); after each apis.api_docs.show_api_doc call add (app_name, api_name); immediately before every first API call assert (app_name, api_name) in inspected_apis, including APIs referenced only in conditional branches.
[shr-00089] For cart-based cost comparisons, treat the live cart as user state, not a disposable scratchpad. Before building any option, snapshot the original cart_items with product_id, quantity, and gift_wrap_quantity plus promo_code/promo_valid. After ordering the chosen option, restore that original state exactly; if the original cart was empty, clear the cart and verify cart_items is empty.
[shr-00094] For Amazon return tasks, build return_items = [(order_id, product_id, quantity_to_return, returned_quantity_before)] and keep_items = [(order_id, product_id, returned_quantity_before)] programmatically from filtered orders; loop over return_items for initiate_return and never pass literal order_id/product_id/deliverer_id/quantity copied from printed output. Derive quantity_to_return = ordered_quantity - returned_quantity, assert it is > 0, exclude the requested keep size, and select deliverer_id from show_return_deliverers by exact normalized name (e.g., UPS) with a deterministic fallback if absent.
[shr-00098] API-doc inspection is task-local and must be mechanically enforced: at the start of every task initialize inspected_apis = set(); do not assume APIs inspected in a previous task remain inspected. Use a helper inspect_api(app, api) that calls apis.api_docs.show_api_doc and adds (app, api), and ensure_inspected(app, api) that asserts membership immediately before every first API call, including supervisor.show_account_passwords, app login APIs, search/mutation APIs, and supervisor.complete_task.
[shr-00106] For any time-window instruction such as 'this week', 'this month', or 'today', derive the current date and all window boundaries programmatically from the authoritative date API. Parse apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time()['date'] into a datetime.date using a month map, compute the window start from that parsed date using the recorded convention, and filter orders by created_at. Never hardcode datetime.date(...) or year/month/day literals copied from printed API output; this applies to Amazon return/order filtering, not only payment-card expiry validation.
[shr-00108] For universal-negative evidence constraints such as 'no one has reported', collect all reviews, questions, and answers without is_verified filters unless the task explicitly says verified. Encode a genuine_reports_by_pid map: a review/answer sentence counts only if it has fade/color-loss context and is not negated; a question alone does not count, but an affirmative answer to a fade-related question counts. Compute clean_candidates = [p for p in verified_candidates if not genuine_reports_by_pid[p['product_id']]], assert clean_candidates, and assign selected_product_id = selected['product_id'] from a deterministic selection over clean_candidates. Never manually choose a product_id after reading evidence output.
[shr-00109] Derive requested size from past orders programmatically: filter past order products to the requested category, collect relative_size values, assert one size or apply a recorded deterministic rule (e.g., most recent order, then most frequent, then normalized alphabetical), and use my_size variable in search_products(relative_size=my_size) and show_product assertions. Do not hardcode size after reading printed order output.
[shr-00117] For 'color fading after washing' universal-negative evidence, require both color-loss context and wash context: count a review/answer as a report only if normalized text contains fade/faded/fading/color loss/colour loss/bleed/run AND wash/laundry/first wash/after washing, or if it is an affirmative answer to an explicit fade-after-wash question. Do not count dye alone, generic quality complaints, or negated statements. This tightens shr-00108 for washing-specific constraints.
[shr-00129] For 'last calendar month' spend/order aggregation, define the window as [first day of previous month, first day of current month) using apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time()['date']; if current month is January, previous month is December of prior year. Filter orders by created_at date in this half-open interval, not by rolling 30 days or by calendar-year logic.
[shr-00132] For 'this or the last calendar month' spend/order aggregation, interpret the phrase as the union of the current and previous calendar months, not rolling 60 days and not only the previous month. Define a half-open window [first day of previous month, first day of next month) from apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time()['date']; handle January and December rollovers; assert window_start <= current_date < window_end; filter orders by created_at date in this interval. Record the convention explicitly in a comment or assertion.
[shr-00140] No-answer action tasks are not exempt from API-doc enforcement: in the final block, call guarded_call('supervisor', 'complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task) or inspect_api/ensure_inspected immediately before apis.supervisor.complete_task(); do not call complete_task bare merely because no answer is required.
[shr-00141] For email RSVP/attendance tasks, select the event thread programmatically from normalized subject/body/event date/participants and never hardcode email_thread_id after printing candidates. If multiple candidate threads exist, read their emails and keep the one matching the requested event/audience before parsing replies. Parse RSVPs by sender email, not display name: exclude the organizer, deduplicate replies by normalized sender email, classify yes/no with explicit affirmative/negative term lists, assert yes_senders is non-empty, and set purchase quantity = len(yes_senders).
[shr-00145] Do not treat emitted code as executed. A task is complete only after the environment returns an observed success response from supervisor.complete_task. If the final block is missing output, is unterminated, or only inspects the API, reissue a properly closed guarded_call('supervisor','complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task) block (with answer only if required), assert the returned dict contains 'message', print it, and only then stop.
[shr-00148] For email RSVP purchase tasks, mechanically lock the event thread before counting replies: assert len(event_matches) == 1; if multiple candidates exist, read all candidate emails and retain only threads whose normalized body contains the requested event terms (e.g., get-together/Saturday/night) and whose organizer/sender email equals the supervisor email, then assert exactly one surviving thread. Do not proceed with subject-only matches or the first candidate.
[shr-00151] Resolve email pronouns and delivery locations from the sender's authoritative contact record. If an email from another person says 'my work place', 'my home', or similar, identify that sender in phone.search_contacts (paginate and match normalized email/relationship) and use the sender's work_address/home_address, not the account owner's saved Amazon address named Work/Home. If Amazon lacks that address, inspect amazon.add_address, add it, re-fetch amazon.show_addresses, and derive selected_address_id by matching normalized street/city/state/country/zip fields.
[shr-00157] For tasks that reference a person by relationship (e.g., my wife's email), resolve that person from phone.search_contacts by relationship, derive their email into a variable, and use that variable in gmail.show_inbox_threads(from_email=...). For today email filtering, parse apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time()[date] into datetime.date and use current_date.isoformat() for min_created_at/max_created_at; never hardcode the email address or date string after printing contacts/date output.
[shr-00158] For emailed purchase instructions without attachments, select the relevant thread programmatically from the filtered inbox by normalized subject/body terms (e.g., amazon/order/buy/category), assert exactly one thread, and derive email_id from thread[email_ids][0] or by looping over email_ids. Parse the email body into requested_category, max_price, rating criterion, and delivery-location variables; use those variables for Amazon search/address selection instead of manually inferring constraints.
[shr-00162] For emailed purchase instructions, parse every numeric constraint from the email body into variables, including requested_quantity: normalize spelled-out numbers ('one', 'two', ...) and digits, assert requested_quantity >= 1, and use that variable in inventory checks, add_product_to_cart quantity, cart/order assertions. Do not hardcode quantity after reading the email.
[shr-00166] For emailed checklist/commitment tasks, resolve the requested audience (e.g., siblings) from phone.search_contacts by relationship, derive normalized emails into a set, and use that set to filter Gmail threads/participants/replies. Do not infer relationships from email participants, display names, or domains.
[shr-00167] Derive checklist thread IDs and checklist email IDs programmatically from normalized subject/body terms, supervisor sender email, and participant emails; assert exactly one checklist email. Use response_to_email_id to identify commitment replies; never hardcode email_thread_id/email_id after printing candidates.
[shr-00173] Interpret 'pick the highest rated ones' in checklist purchase tasks as highest product rating from show_product/search_products, not seller rating, unless the task explicitly says seller. After semantic and inventory filtering, select max by (product rating, lower price, lower product_id) and use selected['product_id'] programmatically.
[shr-00174] For emailed checklist commitments, parse checklist lines into normalized item names and quantities. Treat a reply that names an item as covering the entire checklist line for that item unless the reply explicitly states a partial quantity; if an explicit partial quantity is present, subtract it from the checklist quantity and keep the remainder. Compute remaining items by item-name membership or positive residual quantity, not by requiring the reply quantity to equal the checklist quantity.
[shr-00178] For multi-candidate Amazon checklist purchases, use metadata-first selection: filter/rank candidates from search_products fields (product_type, name, description, rating, price, inventory_quantity), then call show_product only for the selected product or a small top-K set to verify authoritative fields before cart. Do not call show_product for every broad-search candidate because this can exceed request limits; if a request-limit error occurs, reduce verification to top-K/selected only while still deriving product_id programmatically.
[shr-00183] For named-weekday event purchases with a 'by end of day before' deadline, parse apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time()['date'] into datetime.date, compute event_date as the next occurrence of the requested weekday (Saturday=5, Sunday=6, etc.; treat today as the event only when the event is later today), then set delivery_deadline = event_date if event_date == current_date else event_date - datetime.timedelta(days=1). Assert current_date <= delivery_deadline. Use delivery_deadline for both pre-order delivery_days filtering and post-order expected_delivery_at verification; never infer the deadline from printed date strings or hardcode delivery_days <= 1.
[shr-00184] When a purchase quantity is defined by relationship audience (e.g., 'all of my friends and roommates'), derive it from phone.search_contacts: paginate all contacts, filter normalized relationships containing any requested audience term, deduplicate by contact_id, assert num_invitees > 0, and use that variable as cart/order quantity. Do not assume a fixed count or infer audience from email participants/display names.
[shr-00188] If an irreversible purchase or cart restoration has already succeeded but the final supervisor.complete_task call produces no observed USER output, do not repeat place_order, add_product_to_cart, clear_cart, or cart restoration. Reissue only a minimal final block that calls guarded_call('supervisor','complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task) (with answer=variable only if required), asserts isinstance(result, dict) and 'message' in result, prints result, ends with triple backticks followed by a newline, and waits for the environment's success output before stopping. If required variables are missing, re-derive only read-only state such as show_order or show_cart; never reorder.
[shr-00189] When a task requires a monetary total rounded to the nearest whole number (e.g., Amazon wishlist price * quantity), do not use Python round(...) because it uses banker's rounding on exact .5 values. Use Decimal(str(price)) * Decimal(str(quantity)) and quantize(Decimal('1'), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP), then format the integer. For wishlist-to-text tasks, build each line directly from show_wish_list product_name, price, and quantity; ignore tax/delivery unless explicitly requested.
[shr-00193] When a task requires half-up monetary rounding, do not assume the `decimal` module is available; some environments disallow `decimal`. If module availability is uncertain, prefer a no-Decimal integer/string-scaled helper instead of templates that import Decimal. If an existing template imports `decimal` and the import fails, rebuild only the formatting block with the no-Decimal helper; never fall back to Python `round(...)` for final integers because of banker's rounding, and never hardcode printed totals. For Amazon wishlist-to-text tasks, preserve exact `product_name` from `show_wish_list`, ignore tax/delivery unless requested, and only send after the message variable is constructed programmatically.
[shr-00200] For relationship-specified delivery addresses such as 'parents' home address', resolve all matching contacts from phone.search_contacts using exact normalized relationship labels (e.g., 'parent', 'mother', 'father'), deduplicate by contact_id, assert the set is non-empty, and assert all normalized home_address values are identical or apply a recorded deterministic rule if they differ. Derive target_addr from that common address; never use contacts[0] without this consistency check. Then programmatically check amazon.show_addresses for normalized street/city/state/country/zip equality and call amazon.add_address only if the match list is empty; after adding, re-fetch amazon.show_addresses and derive selected_address_id from exactly one normalized match.
[shr-00205] Block-local API-doc preflight must be derived from the code block source, not from runtime execution. Before executing any block, enumerate every (app, api) referenced in that block, including APIs passed to guarded_call inside if/else branches and cart-restoration APIs such as add_gift_wrapping_to_product, apply_promo_code_to_cart, and clear_cart; inspect them all and assert membership before running. Do not rely on guarded_call's lazy inspection because false branches never call it.
[shr-00206] For any Amazon purchase that mutates the live cart, snapshot original cart_items with product_id, quantity, gift_wrap_quantity plus promo_code/promo_valid before adding requested items. After place_order, restore exactly: if original cart was empty, call clear_cart and assert show_cart cart_items == []; if non-empty, re-add items and conditionally reapply gift wrap/promo. This applies to ordinary named-product orders, not only cost comparisons.
[shr-00213] For tasks that deliver the same product to multiple locations or with different gift-wrap states, place separate orders because amazon.place_order applies one address_id and payment_card_id to the entire cart. Snapshot the original cart once before the first order; for each required delivery, rebuild a fresh cart with clear_cart_first=True, set the exact gift_wrap_quantity, verify cart and inventory, place order, and verify order. Restore the original cart only after all orders are complete.
[shr-00218] Mechanical block-source preflight: before executing any code block, scan the block source and build a literal preflight list of every (app, api) appearing in guarded_call('app','api',...) or apis.app.api(...). Call preflight_apis on that list before execution. Do not decide the list from runtime values; conditional APIs such as apply_promo_code_to_cart must be inspected even when orig_promo is None.
[shr-00221] For 'N identical items' split across different addresses or gift-wrap states, lock one selected_product_id and use that exact product_id for every order. Before selection require inventory_quantity >= N; before each order re-fetch show_product and require inventory_quantity >= remaining_units. Do not satisfy 'identical' by choosing different variations, similarly named products, or separate product_ids.
[shr-00224] For plural relationship audiences with distinct delivery addresses (e.g., 'each of my siblings' delivered to their homes), do not collapse addresses into one common address. Paginate phone.search_contacts, filter normalized relationships containing audience terms, deduplicate by contact_id, assert the audience list is non-empty and every contact has a non-empty home_address; parse each home_address, add missing Amazon addresses with unique names, re-fetch show_addresses, derive one address_id per contact by normalized street/city/state/country/zip, assert the address_id count equals audience size and IDs are distinct, then place one identical gift order per address.
[shr-00225] For 'top-rated <category>' product purchases, interpret top-rated as highest product rating unless the task explicitly says seller. Build candidates with exact product_type and inventory_quantity >= audience size, verify candidates with show_product, then select max by (rating, lower price, lower product_id) and lock that one product_id for all split orders.
[shr-00229] For 'top-rated <category>' purchases, do not build the candidate universe from search_products(product_type=category) alone because listings may be mislabeled or use a generic product_type. Union paginated search_products(product_type=category) and search_products(query=category), deduplicate by product_id, verify candidates with show_product, and accept candidates whose product_type matches or whose normalized name/description semantically contains the requested category while excluding accessories/adjacent items. Then filter inventory_quantity >= audience size and select max by (rating, lower price, lower product_id).
[shr-00233] For plural relationship audiences requiring separate home deliveries, implement address resolution as a per-contact existence-gated loop: after parsing each contact home_address, compute matches = [a for a in amazon.show_addresses(...) if addr_match(a, target_addr)]; call amazon.add_address only when matches is empty for that contact, re-fetch show_addresses, and assert exactly one match. Never print show_addresses and then batch-add all audience addresses. Use unique address names and assert the final address_id set size equals audience size.
[shr-00235] Bootstrap API-doc enforcement must precede the first app call: initialize inspected_apis, inspect_api, ensure_inspected, and guarded_call before calling any app API. The first call should be guarded_call('supervisor', 'show_account_passwords', apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords) after inspect_api('supervisor', 'show_account_passwords'). If you call apis.api_docs.show_api_doc directly, immediately add (app, api) to inspected_apis; direct doc printing alone does not satisfy ensure_inspected.
[shr-00236] For personal preference constraints from text messages (e.g., favorite color), parse the question-answer pair rather than scanning all messages for any matching keyword. Locate the supervisor's message containing the preference question, then parse the recipient's reply with a context regex such as 'it is ([a-z]+)' or 'my favorite color is ([a-z]+)' after normalizing punctuation; assert the extracted value is non-empty and use it as a variable. Do not accept a color word from unrelated messages.
[shr-00240] For personal preference constraints from phone voice messages (e.g., favorite color), parse the question-answer pair from voice messages, not text messages or keyword scans. Paginate phone.search_voice_messages for the recipient, locate the supervisor's message containing the preference question, then parse the recipient's reply with sent_at > question.sent_at using a context regex such as 'it is ([a-z]+)' or 'my favorite color is ([a-z]+)'; assert the extracted value is non-empty and use it as a variable. Do not accept a color word from unrelated voice messages.
[shr-00247] For color-specific category purchases (e.g., favorite-color sweater), build the candidate universe from category searches (product_type and query), not from color-filtered searches. Filter candidates by product_type/name/description and inventory_quantity >= 1 from search metadata, then verify all remaining in-stock candidates with show_product, or if too many use a documented top-K rule. Derive color candidates from both authoritative main color and authoritative variation color, verify each color product_id with show_product, then select max by (rating, lower price, lower product_id) and lock that product_id.
[shr-00249] For purchase instructions from phone text messages, resolve the named sender from paginated phone.search_contacts by exact normalized first_name (and last_name when available), assert exactly one contact, then paginate all phone.search_text_messages with that contact's phone_number. Locate the recommendation message programmatically by normalized keywords such as buy/recommend/cheaper/quoted product names, extract quoted product names with a parser, assert exactly one recommendation message and the expected number of candidates, and parse the selection rule (e.g., 'pick the cheaper one') into a variable. If the user task also names a product category (e.g., 'food scale'), enforce that category as a hard constraint on the final selected product unless the message explicitly overrides it: filter candidates by authoritative show_product name/description/category, select the unique category-valid candidate if only one exists, otherwise apply the parsed selection rule among category-valid candidates; never order a non-category item merely because it is cheaper.
[shr-00254] For unspecified Amazon address, exhaust default discovery before any fallback: inspect and call supervisor.show_addresses and amazon.show_account, assert/record that neither exposes default_address_id or an equivalent default address field, then derive selected_address_id from amazon.show_addresses using exact normalized name 'Home' if present (assert exactly one match), otherwise lowest address_id; assert selected_address_id is in amazon.show_addresses before place_order. Do not choose lowest address_id after checking only amazon.show_account or supervisor.show_payment_cards.
[shr-00255] Message-driven relationship tasks: when the task says 'my roommate/friend asked' but does not name the person, do not assert exactly one contact with that relationship. Paginate phone.search_contacts, build audience_phone_set from all contacts whose normalized relationships contain the requested term, then paginate phone.search_text_messages for each audience phone. Filter instruction messages by sender.phone_number in audience_phone_set and sender.phone_number != supervisor_phone, require the requested action terms (e.g., return plus venmo/money/send), assert exactly one instruction message, and derive requester_contact/requester_phone/requester_email from that message sender. Parse product names and refund instructions from the message into variables; never choose the requester by contact order or relationship uniqueness.
[shr-00256] Venmo refund amount source: when a message says 'venmo me order total cost I sent you for it' or otherwise refers to money already sent, do not default to Amazon paid_amount. After resolving requester_email and supervisor_email, inspect and paginate venmo.show_transactions, filter transactions where sender.email equals requester_email and receiver.email equals supervisor_email. If matching payment(s) exist, derive refund_amount from the authoritative payment: prefer payments whose description contains the returned product or order_id, then prefer amount equal to Amazon order total within cent tolerance, then latest created_at, then lowest transaction_id. Only if no matching Venmo payment exists, record that fact and fall back to Amazon paid_amount. Use refund_amount variable in venmo.create_transaction; never hardcode amount.
[shr-00262] For message-driven Amazon return/refund tasks, do not select the returned order solely as the latest exact product match when multiple matching orders exist. Build return_candidates with order_id, product_id, quantity_to_return, paid_amount, created_at, and address_text. First prefer candidates whose normalized address_text contains the requester's normalized home_address from phone.search_contacts, or whose Venmo payment description contains the returned product/order_id. If no requester-specific evidence exists, apply latest created_at then lowest order_id and record that deterministic fallback.
[shr-00265] Final completion gate: after all irreversible actions, do not stop or write prose until the environment shows USER output from supervisor.complete_task containing 'message'. If the final block produced no USER output, immediately reissue only a minimal guarded_call('supervisor','complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task) block (with answer only if required), assert isinstance(result, dict) and 'message' in result, print result, and close the code block; never repeat place_order, initiate_return, create_transaction, cart restoration, or messaging.
[shr-00266] Venmo refund absence proof: for 'venmo me money I sent you' tasks, prove absence before falling back to Amazon paid_amount by running both a filtered search (user_email=requester, direction='received') and an unfiltered full pagination, then manually filtering sender.email=requester and receiver.email=supervisor in both. Only if both result sets are empty may refund_amount=amazon_total; record the zero counts and use a description containing order_id and returned product name.
[shr-00268] For 'delivered today' Amazon package tasks, filter order_items by expected_delivery_at date == current_date, not created_at or delivered_at. Paginate all orders, collect every matching item into today_items = [(order_id, product_id, ordered_quantity, expected_delivery_at, paid_amount, address_id), ...], assert len(today_items) == 1 before using order-level paid_amount; if multiple, diagnose or compute item-level amount. Never break after the first match.
[shr-00269] For Venmo 'request them the money' tasks, use venmo.create_payment_request rather than create_transaction unless the task says send/pay. Derive receiver_email from the named contact's email and venmo.search_users exact normalized email; derive amount from the authoritative Amazon order paid_amount variable (or a recorded prior-payment source if the task says money was already sent); include order_id/product in description; verify with venmo.show_sent_payment_requests before complete_task.
[shr-00275] For named-person delivered-today package tasks, treat recipient identity as a hard verification gate, not a ranking heuristic. After filtering order_items by expected_delivery_at == current_date, require exactly one order whose address_text/address name matches the named contact's home_address or an Amazon address containing that name; assert exactly one contact by exact normalized first_name. If no positive match or multiple candidates exist, stop and diagnose; never fall back to delivered_at, latest created_at, highest score, or arbitrary contact_id before Venmo.
[shr-00279] For supervisor-stated recipient tasks such as 'the package delivered today was an order for Edwin', use a recorded precedence rule: treat the supervisor's explicit association as authoritative only after mechanically exhausting available recipient evidence and asserting uniqueness. Check exactly one today item, exactly one named contact, order address_text/address_id/name, Amazon address names, contact home/work addresses, and show_product name/description for recipient clues. If positive evidence exists, use it. If no positive evidence exists but there is no contradictory evidence and the task explicitly names the recipient, record that the supervisor premise is authoritative and proceed; do not invent ad hoc heuristics such as coworker/work-address matching. If multiple today items, multiple named contacts, or contradictory evidence exists, stop and diagnose.
[shr-00282] Reset-cart tasks are an explicit exception to cart-preservation rules. If the instruction says reset/replace the cart, do not snapshot and restore the original cart after mutating it. Build a cart_plan of only available checklist items, clear the existing cart by adding the first planned item with clear_cart_first=True (or call clear_cart if cart_plan is empty), verify the final cart exactly equals cart_plan, and leave the cart in that reset state. Apply cart-restoration templates only when the task requires preserving user state after ordering/comparison, not when the desired end state is the new cart.
[shr-00289] For simple_note checklist or shopping-list tasks, resolve the note_id programmatically from paginated search_notes by normalized title terms such as amazon, shopping, list, or purchase. If multiple title candidates exist, call show_note on each and keep the one whose content contains the requested list markers; assert exactly one selected note and derive note_id from matches[0]['note_id']. Never hardcode note_id after printing candidates, and parse show_note content into requested_specs with assertions before Amazon product resolution.
[shr-00291] For Amazon Prime membership spend questions such as 'how much have I paid in prime membership since I made the amazon account', use amazon.show_account.registered_at as the authoritative account-creation lower bound and amazon.show_prime_subscriptions as the authoritative payment records. Paginate subscriptions, deduplicate by prime_subscription_id, validate each record has start_date and numeric paid_amount, parse both registered_at and start_date into datetime.date using the date portion before T or whitespace, include subscriptions with parsed start_date >= parsed registered_at, and sum rounded paid_amount. ## APIs TO USE FOR SPECIFIC INFORMATION
[api-00004] You can use the "supervisor" app to get information about my accounts and use the "phone" app to get information about friends and family.

[api-00013] Amazon purchase API schemas: search_products returns a list of product dicts with product_id, seller_id, product_type, name, price, inventory_quantity, rating, variations; show_seller returns a dict with seller_id, name, rating; show_payment_cards returns a list of dicts with payment_card_id, card_name; show_addresses returns a list of dicts with address_id, name; add_product_to_cart returns a message dict; show_cart returns a cart dict with cart_items; place_order returns a dict with order_id and message; show_order returns an order dict with address_id, payment_card_id, and order_items.
[api-00017] For highest-rated seller tasks, do not treat seller_ids seen in search_products as the complete seller universe. Also enumerate candidate sellers for the category using search_sellers and/or show_product_feature_choices(product_type=...), cross-check with product seller_ids, fetch authoritative ratings with show_seller, and break seller-rating ties deterministically (e.g., lowest seller_id).
[api-00020] Amazon category-purchase schema clarifications: supervisor.show_account_passwords returns a list of {'account_name', 'password'}; amazon.login returns {'access_token', 'token_type'}; search_products and show_product include name and description, which should be used for semantic validation; show_product_feature_choices(product_type=...) returns {'colors': [...], 'relative_sizes': [...], 'sellers': [{'seller_id': int, 'name': str}]} and does NOT include seller rating, so call show_seller for each candidate seller_id to get authoritative rating; show_cart returns cart_items with quantity; show_order returns order_items with ordered_quantity.
[api-00024] Amazon payment-card schema clarification: show_payment_cards returns payment_card_id, card_name, owner_name, card_number, expiry_year, expiry_month, and cvv_number. When no payment card is specified, do not select by arbitrary ID; validate expiry_year/expiry_month before place_order.
[api-00031] Amazon search_products rating filters: for minimum product/seller rating tasks, pass min_product_rating and min_seller_rating (with product_type/query) to reduce noise, but treat these filters as prefilter only; still verify seller ratings with show_seller and product details with show_product.
[api-00034] Amazon wishlist schema: show_wish_list returns a list of dicts with product_id, product_name, quantity, price; it does NOT include product_type, inventory_quantity, length/width/height, or variations. For wishlist items with physical constraints, derive candidate product_ids from product_name, then call show_product(product_id=...) for each candidate to get authoritative dimensions, inventory, product_type, name/description, and variations.
[api-00044] Amazon show_product full schema: returns product_id, seller_id, product_type, name, description, price, delivery_days, inventory_quantity, weight, length, width, height, color, relative_size, rating, num_product_reviews, num_product_questions, and variations (list of dicts with product_id, relative_size, color). For physical-fit wishlist purchases, use length/width for fit (allow swapped orientation only if task does not fix orientation), product_type/name/description for semantic validation, and inventory_quantity for stock.
[api-00047] Amazon product evidence API schemas: show_product_reviews(product_id=..., is_verified=True, page_index=..., page_limit=...) returns a list of review dicts with review_id, product_id, is_verified, rating, title, text, created_at, and user{name,email}. show_product_questions(product_id=..., page_index=..., page_limit=...) returns a list of question dicts with question_id, question, created_at, and user{name,email}; it does NOT include an is_verified flag. show_product_question_answers(question_id=..., is_verified=True, page_index=..., page_limit=...) returns a list of answer dicts with question_answer_id, question_id, is_verified, answer, created_at, and user{name,email}. Verified-purchaser status must be checked on reviews and answers, not on questions.
[api-00056] Amazon evidence product/API schema clarification: search_products may return accessories or variant listings; show_product.variations should not be used for evidence unless requested. Normalize product names (lowercase, hyphens to spaces) to match variants such as 'e-reader', 'eReader', and 'e reader'. Evidence endpoints return lists directly; inspect docs for access_token requirements and check for error dicts.
[api-00058] Amazon search_products evidence schema: the documented parameters include query, product_type, color, relative_size, price/rating filters, seller_id, page_index, page_limit, and sort_by, but NOT access_token. For product evidence tasks, call search_products without access_token unless the current API doc explicitly lists it; narrow device/laptop searches by adding product_type (e.g., 'laptop') along with the exact query to reduce irrelevant accessories and unrelated categories, then still derive the product_id programmatically from exact normalized name matches.
[api-00062] Amazon cart-state schema and restoration APIs: show_cart returns a dict with promo_code, promo_valid, and cart_items; each cart_item includes product_id, product_name, delivery_days, quantity, price, gift_wrap_quantity. show_order order_items include ordered_quantity and gift_wrap_quantity. Relevant mutation APIs include clear_cart, add_product_to_cart, add_gift_wrapping_to_product, remove_gift_wrapping_from_product, apply_promo_code_to_cart, and remove_promo_code_from_cart. To revert a cart exactly, snapshot per-item gift_wrap_quantity and cart-level promo_code/promo_valid, inspect the mutation API docs, re-add items, reapply gift wrapping/promo code when present, and verify full cart equality rather than only product_id->quantity.
[api-00067] Phone current-date schema: apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time() returns {'date': 'Weekday, Month Day, Year', 'time': 'HH:MM AM/PM'} and requires no access_token. Use it as the authoritative current date for payment-card expiry validation before falling back to latest-expiry heuristics.
[api-00068] Amazon financial cart/order schema: show_cart returns delivery_fee, base_cost, tax, gift_wrap_fee, discount, total_cost, promo_code, promo_valid, and cart_items; show_order returns tax, delivery_fee, gift_wrap_fee, discount, paid_amount, and order_items with price, ordered_quantity, and gift_wrap_quantity.
[api-00077] Amazon named-product schema: search_products/show_product can return a product_type that is a generic category different from the requested title, e.g. 'Athleta Studio Wrap' may have product_type 'yoga pants'. For explicitly named products, exact normalized name match plus show_product name assertion is authoritative; do not reject solely because product_type is not the guessed category, but still verify inventory and derive product_id programmatically.
[api-00081] Amazon gift-wrap mutation schema: add_gift_wrapping_to_product(product_id=..., access_token=..., quantity=...) and remove_gift_wrapping_from_product(...) return a message dict; assert the success message, then verify show_cart cart_items gift_wrap_quantity reflects the intended wrapping state.
[api-00086] Amazon gift-wrap auxiliary search schema: search_products(query='gift wrap') can return unrelated items whose names contain 'wrap' (e.g., socks, yoga pants). For separate wrapping paper, use product_type='gift wrap' and/or require normalized name/description to contain 'gift wrap' or 'wrapping paper'; then filter inventory_quantity >= needed quantity and choose cheapest programmatically.
[api-00090] Amazon/supervisor payment-card and address schemas: supervisor.show_payment_cards returns a list of dicts with owner_name, card_name, card_number, expiry_year, expiry_month, and cvv_number but no payment_card_id; supervisor.show_addresses returns a list of dicts with name, street_address, city, state, country, and zip_code but no address_id. Amazon.show_payment_cards and Amazon.show_addresses provide the IDs required by place_order. Query supervisor APIs for default/profile fields when checking unspecified card/address, but derive selected_payment_card_id and selected_address_id from Amazon lists.
[api-00093] Amazon return/order schema: show_orders returns a list of order dicts with order_id, created_at, and order_items; each order_item includes product_id, ordered_quantity, returned_quantity, gift_wrap_quantity, price, expected_delivery_at, and delivered_at. show_return_deliverers returns a list of {'deliverer_id', 'name'} and takes no parameters. initiate_return(order_id=..., product_id=..., deliverer_id=..., quantity=..., access_token=...) returns {'message', 'return_id'}; quantity should be the remaining returnable quantity (ordered_quantity - returned_quantity). show_order returns order_items with returned_quantity for post-return verification. If query and sort_by are both provided, show_orders is relevance-ranked first, so for time-window tasks paginate with sort_by='-created_at' and filter created_at explicitly.
[api-00102] Phone/Simple Note messaging schema: phone.search_contacts returns a list of contact dicts with contact_id, first_name, last_name, email, phone_number, relationships; phone.search_text_messages returns a list of message dicts with text_message_id, sender{name, phone_number}, message, sent_at; phone.send_text_message(access_token=..., phone_number=..., message=...) returns a message dict; simple_note.search_notes returns note metadata (note_id, title, tags, created_at) without content, while simple_note.show_note(note_id=..., access_token=...) returns the note dict including content.
[api-00103] Spotify playlist/song schema: show_playlist_library returns a list of playlist dicts including playlist_id; show_playlist(playlist_id=..., access_token=...) returns a dict with songs list containing song id; show_song(song_id=...) returns a dict with song_id, title, like_count. For most-liked tasks, collect unique song ids, fetch show_song for each, select max by like_count with deterministic tie-breaker, and pass the selected song's title variable to complete_task.
[api-00110] Amazon search_products relative_size schema: relative_size accepts one of ['extra-small', 'small', 'medium', 'large', 'extra-large']; derive my_size from past order show_product['relative_size'] and pass it directly after normalization.
[api-00121] Amazon show_account schema: returns first_name, last_name, email, registered_at, last_logged_in, verified, is_prime; it may not include default_payment_card_id or default_address_id. Call it before unspecified-card/address fallback and assert absence of default fields, then derive IDs from amazon.show_payment_cards/show_addresses.
[api-00126] Amazon show_orders financial aggregation schema: show_orders returns a list of order dicts that include order_id, created_at, paid_amount, tax, delivery_fee, gift_wrap_fee, discount, address_id, payment_card_id, and order_items. For 'how much did I spend' tasks, sum order-level paid_amount after deduplicating by order_id; do not assume only show_order exposes paid_amount.
[api-00130] Amazon show_orders spend aggregation: the documented response is a list of order dicts containing order_id, created_at, and paid_amount. Paginate with page_index/page_limit and sort_by='-created_at' when documented. If the live response is a dict, accept it only when the success schema documents a list-valued key; extract that key and assert it is a list. Parse created_at by taking the date portion before 'T' or whitespace; do not assume ISO 'T'.
[api-00135] Gmail/file_system email-attachment schema: gmail.show_inbox_threads returns a list of thread dicts with email_thread_id, email_ids, subject, participants (list of {'name','email'}), created_at; inspect docs for page_index/page_limit and paginate until an empty page when documented. gmail.show_email(email_id=..., access_token=...) returns a dict with subject, body, sender, recipients, and attachments list containing {'id','file_name'}. gmail.download_attachment(attachment_id=..., file_system_access_token=..., access_token=..., download_to_file_path=..., overwrite=...) returns {'message','file_path'} and requires a file_system login token. file_system.login returns {'access_token','token_type'}; file_system.show_file(file_path=..., access_token=...) returns {'file_id','path','content','created_at','updated_at'}. Use file content as the authoritative checklist source.
[api-00146] Supervisor completion schema: apis.supervisor.complete_task() returns a dict containing 'message' for successful completion. For no-answer action tasks call it without answer; for answer tasks call complete_task(answer=<variable>). Always inspect the API doc and assert isinstance(result, dict) and 'message' in result before treating the task as done.

Use a length-agnostic AMZ-prefix/context validation such as AMZ followed by one or more alphanumeric characters unless the API documentation explicitly guarantees a fixed length.

[code-00023] Robust highest-rated-seller category selection with semantic filtering and deterministic tie-breakers:
category = 'portable air conditioner'
products = []
page_index = 0
while True:
    page = apis.amazon.search_products(product_type=category, page_index=page_index, page_limit=20)
    if not page:
        break
    products.extend(page)
    page_index += 1
feature_choices = apis.amazon.show_product_feature_choices(product_type=category)
seller_ids = sorted({p['seller_id'] for p in products} | {s['seller_id'] for s in feature_choices['sellers']})
sellers = [apis.amazon.show_seller(seller_id=sid) for sid in seller_ids]
best_seller = max(sellers, key=lambda s: (s['rating'], -s['seller_id']))
# Replace keywords/bad_terms for other requested categories.
keywords = ['air conditioner']
bad_terms = ['tv', 'television']
def semantic_match(p):
    text = (p.get('name', '') + ' ' + p.get('description', '')).lower()
    return any(k in text for k in keywords) and not any(b in text for b in bad_terms)
candidates = [p for p in products if p['seller_id'] == best_seller['seller_id'] and p['inventory_quantity'] > 0 and semantic_match(p)]
assert candidates
selected = max(candidates, key=lambda p: (p['rating'], -p['price'], -p['product_id']))
assert selected['product_type'] == category
print(best_seller, selected['product_id'], selected['name'])
[code-00036] Programmatic wishlist fit selection (replace category_terms and max_l/max_w):
wishlist = apis.amazon.show_wish_list(access_token=amazon_access_token)
category_terms = ['dish rack']
candidate_ids = [w['product_id'] for w in wishlist if any(term in w['product_name'].lower() for term in category_terms)]
assert candidate_ids
max_l, max_w = 15.3, 13.3
def fits(p):
    return p['inventory_quantity'] > 0 and ((p['length'] <= max_l and p['width'] <= max_w) or (p['length'] <= max_w and p['width'] <= max_l))
def semantic(p):
    text = (p.get('name', '') + ' ' + p.get('description', '')).lower()
    return any(term in text for term in category_terms)
seen = set()
fits_products = []
for pid in candidate_ids:
    p = apis.amazon.show_product(product_id=pid)
    if p['product_id'] not in seen and semantic(p) and fits(p):
        seen.add(p['product_id'])
        fits_products.append(p)
    # Inspect variations only when variations are allowed; if not, remove this loop or require variation_id in wishlist_ids.
    for v in p.get('variations', []):
        vid = v['product_id']
        if vid in seen:
            continue
        vp = apis.amazon.show_product(product_id=vid)
        if semantic(vp) and fits(vp):
            seen.add(vid)
            fits_products.append(vp)
assert fits_products
selected = min(fits_products, key=lambda x: (x['price'], -x['rating'], x['product_id']))
selected_product_id = selected['product_id']
print(selected_product_id, selected['name'])

    while True:
        page = apis.amazon.show_product_question_answers(question_id=q['question_id'], is_verified=True, page_index=page_index, page_limit=20)
        if not page:
            break
        verified_answers.extend(page)
        page_index += 1

def parse_hours(text):
    text = text.lower()
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isdigit():
            j = i
            while j < len(text) and text[j].isdigit():
                j += 1
            num = int(text[i:j])
            rest = text[j:].lstrip()
            if rest.startswith('hour'):
                return num
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    return None

evidence = []
for r in verified_reviews:
    h = parse_hours(r['title'] + ' ' + r['text'])
    if h is not None:
        evidence.append((r['created_at'], h, 'review', r['review_id']))
for a in verified_answers:
    h = parse_hours(a['answer'])
    if h is not None:
        evidence.append((a['created_at'], h, 'answer', a['question_answer_id']))
assert evidence
evidence.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
answer = evidence[0][1]
print(product_id, answer, evidence)

print(apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='supervisor', api_name='complete_task'))
apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=answer)
[code-00057] # Battery-hour context parser (use with verified reviews/answers only).
import re
def norm_name(s): return ' '.join(str(s or '').lower().replace('-', ' ').split())
def battery_hours(text):
    t = norm_name(text)
    if not any(k in t for k in ('battery', 'power', 'charge', 'charging')): return None
    m = re.search(r'(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*(?:hours?|hrs?)', t)
    return float(m.group(1)) if m else None
# For Q&A, pass question + answer so battery-related questions count.

[code-00069] def parse_current_ym(date_str):
    month_map = {'january': 1, 'february': 2, 'march': 3, 'april': 4, 'may': 5, 'june': 6, 'july': 7, 'august': 8, 'september': 9, 'october': 10, 'november': 11, 'december': 12}
    parts = [p.strip() for p in date_str.split(',')]
    assert len(parts) == 3, date_str
    month = month_map[parts[1].split()[0].lower()]
    year = int(parts[2])
    return year, month

# After inspecting docs for amazon.show_payment_cards and phone.get_current_date_and_time:

if visa:
    selected_payment_card_id = min(c['payment_card_id'] for c in visa)
else:
    assert len(visa) == 0
    # Inspect amazon.show_account and supervisor.show_payment_cards for default_payment_card_id; record absence.
    current_ym = parse_current_ym(apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time()['date'])
    valid = [c for c in cards if (int(c['expiry_year']), int(c['expiry_month'])) >= current_ym]
    assert valid
    max_expiry = max((int(c['expiry_year']), int(c['expiry_month'])) for c in valid)
    selected_payment_card_id = min(c['payment_card_id'] for c in valid if (int(c['expiry_year']), int(c['expiry_month'])) == max_expiry)
assert selected_payment_card_id in {c['payment_card_id'] for c in cards}
[code-00079] # Named multi-quantity product ID derivation (use with cart-preserving order template):
def norm(s): return ' '.join(str(s or '').lower().replace('-', ' ').split())
def exact_product_id(query, target):
    products = []
    page_index = 0
    while True:
        page = apis.amazon.search_products(query=query, page_index=page_index, page_limit=20)
        assert isinstance(page, list), page
        if not page:
            break
        products.extend(page)
        page_index += 1
    matches = [p for p in products if norm(p['name']) == norm(target)]
    assert len(matches) == 1, (target, matches)
    return matches[0]['product_id']

requested_specs = [('Ascend Ultralight Hammock', 1), ('The Complete Mediterranean Cookbook', 3), ('Athleta Studio Wrap', 3)]
requested_items = []
for target, qty in requested_specs:
    pid = exact_product_id(target, target)
    p = apis.amazon.show_product(product_id=pid)
    assert norm(p['name']) == norm(target), p['name']
    assert p['inventory_quantity'] >= qty, (pid, p['inventory_quantity'], qty)
    requested_items.append((pid, qty))
# Use requested_items, not literal IDs, for add_product_to_cart, cart/order verification, and restoration.

import datetime
def parse_current_date(date_str):
    month_map = {'january': 1, 'february': 2, 'march': 3, 'april': 4, 'may': 5, 'june': 6, 'july': 7, 'august': 8, 'september': 9, 'october': 10, 'november': 11, 'december': 12}

[code-00123] Guarded API-call helper to prevent inspection/call mismatches (extends code-00105):
inspected_apis = set()
def inspect_api(app, api):
    doc = apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name=app, api_name=api)
    inspected_apis.add((app, api))
    return doc
def ensure_inspected(app, api):
    assert (app, api) in inspected_apis, (app, api)
def guarded_call(app, api, fn, **kwargs):
    if (app, api) not in inspected_apis:
        inspect_api(app, api)
    ensure_inspected(app, api)
    return fn(**kwargs)
# Example: cart = guarded_call('amazon', 'show_cart', apis.amazon.show_cart, access_token=amazon_access_token)
# If the API was inspected in a previous block, guarded_call still executes ensure_inspected in the current block before the first call.

def parse_current_date(date_str):
    month_map = {'january':1,'february':2,'march':3,'april':4,'may':5,'june':6,'july':7,'august':8,'september':9,'october':10,'november':11,'december':12}
    parts = [p.strip() for p in date_str.split(',')]
    assert len(parts) == 3, date_str
    month = month_map[parts[1].split()[0].lower()]
    day = int(parts[1].split()[1])
    year = int(parts[2])
    return datetime.date(year, month, day)
def first_of_previous_month(d):
    if d.month == 1:
        return datetime.date(d.year - 1, 12, 1)
    return datetime.date(d.year, d.month - 1, 1)
def first_of_next_month(d):
    if d.month == 12:
        return datetime.date(d.year + 1, 1, 1)
    return datetime.date(d.year, d.month + 1, 1)
def order_date(created_at):
    date_part = created_at.split('T')[0].split()[0]
    y, m, d = [int(x) for x in date_part.split('-')]
    return datetime.date(y, m, d)

WORD_NUM = {'one':1,'two':2,'three':3,'four':4,'five':5,'six':6,'seven':7,'eight':8,'nine':9,'ten':10}
def clean_word(w): return ''.join(ch for ch in w.lower() if ch.isalnum())
def parse_requested_quantity(body):
    for w in str(body).lower().split():
        cw = clean_word(w)
        if cw.isdigit(): return int(cw)
        if cw in WORD_NUM: return WORD_NUM[cw]
    return 1
requested_quantity = parse_requested_quantity(body)
assert requested_quantity >= 1

        ensure_inspected(app, api)

def paginate_search(**kwargs):
    products = []
    page_index = 0
    while True:
        page = guarded_call('amazon', 'search_products', apis.amazon.search_products, page_index=page_index, page_limit=20, **kwargs)
        assert isinstance(page, list), page
        if not page:
            break
        products.extend(page)
        page_index += 1
    return products
def select_best_checklist_item(item, qty):
    raw = paginate_search(product_type=item) + paginate_search(query=item)
    by_pid = {}
    for p in raw:
        by_pid[p['product_id']] = p
    verified = []
    for p in by_pid.values():
        full = guarded_call('amazon', 'show_product', apis.amazon.show_product, product_id=p['product_id'])
        assert isinstance(full, dict) and 'message' not in full, full
        text = norm(full.get('name', '') + ' ' + full.get('description', ''))
        type_match = full.get('product_type') == item or norm(full.get('product_type', '')) == norm(item)
        semantic_match = norm(item) in text
        if (type_match or semantic_match) and full.get('inventory_quantity', 0) >= qty:
            verified.append(full)
    assert verified, (item, qty, len(by_pid))
    best = max(verified, key=lambda x: (x['rating'], -x['price'], -x['product_id']))
    return best['product_id'], qty, best['name'], best['rating'], best['inventory_quantity']
selected_items = [select_best_checklist_item(item, qty) for item, qty in remaining]

def parse_current_date(date_str):

def next_weekday(current_date, target_weekday):
    days = (target_weekday - current_date.weekday()) % 7
    return current_date + datetime.timedelta(days=days)

def half_up_whole(price, quantity):
    return int((Decimal(str(price)) * Decimal(str(quantity))).quantize(Decimal('1'), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP))

[code-00194] No-Decimal half-up wishlist-to-text template (use when decimal is disallowed or module availability is uncertain):
def half_up_whole_no_decimal(price, quantity):
    s = str(price).strip()
    if 'e' in s.lower():
        total = float(s) * int(quantity)
        return int(total + 0.5 + 1e-9)
    sign = -1 if s.startswith('-') else 1
    if sign < 0:
        s = s[1:]
    if '.' in s:
        whole, frac = s.split('.', 1)
    else:
        whole, frac = s, ''
    whole = whole or '0'
    frac = frac.rstrip('0')
    digits = whole + frac
    scale = 10 ** len(frac)
    value = int(digits or '0') * int(quantity)
    return sign * int((2 * value + scale) // (2 * scale))

    quantity = item['quantity']
    assert isinstance(product_name, str) and product_name.strip(), item
    assert isinstance(price, (int, float)), item
    assert isinstance(quantity, int) and quantity >= 1, item
    message_lines.append(f'{product_name} => ${half_up_whole_no_decimal(price, quantity)}')
assert len(message_lines) == len(wish_list)
message = '\n'.join(message_lines)
[code-00199] def coerce_whole_quantity(q):
    assert isinstance(q, (int, float)), q
    if isinstance(q, float):
        assert q.is_integer(), q
        q = int(q)
    assert q >= 1, q
    return q

def get_password(passwords, app_name):
    assert isinstance(passwords, list), passwords
    matching = [p for p in passwords if p.get('account_name') == app_name]

    return matching[0]['password']

[code-00298] # Spotify premium membership total aggregation (requires guarded_call from code-00123, get_password from code-00219, and spotify_access_token from spotify.login).
import datetime
def parse_dt(s):
    text = str(s).strip()
    if text.endswith('Z'):
        text = text[:-1] + '+00:00'
    try:
        return datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(text)
    except ValueError:
        date_part = text.split('T')[0].split()[0]
        y, m, d = [int(x) for x in date_part.split('-')]
        return datetime.datetime(y, m, d)

account = guarded_call('spotify', 'show_account', apis.spotify.show_account, access_token=spotify_access_token)
assert isinstance(account, dict) and 'registered_at' in account, account
registered_dt = parse_dt(account['registered_at'])
subs = []
page_index = 0
while True:
    page = guarded_call('spotify', 'show_premium_subscriptions', apis.spotify.show_premium_subscriptions, access_token=spotify_access_token, page_index=page_index, page_limit=20)
    assert isinstance(page, list), page
    if not page:
        break
    subs.extend(page)
    page_index += 1
seen = set()
valid = []
for s in subs:
    assert isinstance(s, dict) and 'premium_subscription_id' in s and 'start_date' in s and 'paid_amount' in s, s
    sid = s['premium_subscription_id']
    if sid in seen:
        continue
    seen.add(sid)
    assert isinstance(s['paid_amount'], (int, float)), s
    if parse_dt(s['start_date']) >= registered_dt:
        valid.append(s)
total_paid = round(sum(float(s['paid_amount']) for s in valid), 2)
# Date convention: full datetime comparison; fallback treats date-only strings as midnight.
result = guarded_call('supervisor', 'complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task, answer=total_paid)
assert isinstance(result, dict) and 'message' in result, result
print(result)

import datetime
def parse_sub_dt(s):
    text = str(s).strip()
    if text.endswith('Z'):
        text = text[:-1]
    try:
        return datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(text)
    except ValueError:
        date_part = text.split('T')[0].split()[0]
        y, m, d = [int(x) for x in date_part.split('-')]
        return datetime.datetime(y, m, d)
def last4(v):
    if isinstance(v, (int, float)):
        s = format(v, '.0f') if isinstance(v, float) else str(v)
    else:
        s = str(v)
    if 'e' in s.lower():
        try:
            s = format(float(s), '.0f')
        except Exception:
            pass
    digits = ''.join(ch for ch in s if ch.isdigit())
    assert len(digits) >= 4, (v, digits)
    return digits[-4:]

[code-00308] import datetime
def parse_sub_dt(s):
    text = str(s).strip()
    if text.endswith('Z'):
        text = text[:-1]
    try:
        return datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(text)
    except ValueError:
        date_part = text.split('T')[0].split()[0]
        y, m, d = [int(x) for x in date_part.split('-')]
        return datetime.datetime(y, m, d)
def last4(v):
    if isinstance(v, (int, float)):
        s = format(v, '.0f') if isinstance(v, float) else str(v)
    else:
        s = str(v)
    if 'e' in s.lower():
        try:
            s = format(float(s), '.0f')
        except Exception:
            pass
    digits = ''.join(ch for ch in s if ch.isdigit())
    assert len(digits) >= 4, (v, digits)
    return digits[-4:]

        break
    subs.extend(page)
    page_index += 1

def parse_bill(content):
    total = None
    card_last4 = None
    payer = None
    for line in content.splitlines():
        s = line.strip()
        if s.startswith('Payer Name'):
            payer = s.split('=>', 1)[1].strip()
        elif s.startswith('Total'):
            assert '$' in s, s
            total = float(s.split('$', 1)[1].strip())
        elif s.startswith('Paid by Card'):
            digits = ''.join(ch for ch in s if ch.isdigit())
            assert len(digits) >= 4, s
            card_last4 = digits[-4:]
    assert total is not None and card_last4 is not None, content
    return payer, total, card_last4

[code-00326] # Safe hotel-room confirmation classifier (requires norm from existing templates).
def is_hotel_confirmation(e):
    text = norm(str(e.get('subject','')) + ' ' + str(e.get('body','')) + ' ' + str(e.get('sender',{}).get('name','')) + ' ' + str(e.get('sender',{}).get('email','')))
    lodging_terms = ['room booking', 'hotel', 'resort', 'inn', 'suites', 'manor', 'stay', 'accommodation']
    strong_exclude = ['flight', 'airline', 'boarding pass', 'amazon order', 'shopping with us', 'happy flying']
    return any(term in text for term in lodging_terms) and not any(term in text for term in strong_exclude)

# Requires norm, guarded_call, preflight_apis, inspect_api, ensure_inspected, get_password, passwords, supervisor_email, gmail_access_token, fs_access_token, phone_access_token, all_contacts, threads.
def parse_current_date(date_str):
    month_map = {'january':1,'february':2,'march':3,'april':4,'may':5,'june':6,'july':7,'august':8,'september':9,'october':10,'november':11,'december':12}
    parts = [p.strip() for p in date_str.split(',')]
    assert len(parts) == 3, date_str
    month = month_map[parts[1].split()[0].lower()]
    day = int(parts[1].split()[1])
    year = int(parts[2])
    return datetime.date(year, month, day)
def parse_current_time(time_str):
    return datetime.datetime.strptime(time_str, '%I:%M %p').time()

[code-00393] No-csv schedule reminder helpers: parse schedule without csv, preserve exact emails, derive template path programmatically, and verify scheduled_send_at exactly. Use with file_system.show_directory(substring=..., entry_type='files', recursive=True).
```python
import datetime

def parse_schedule_no_csv(content):
    lines = content.splitlines()
    assert lines, content
    header = [h.strip().lower() for h in lines[0].split(',', 2)]
    required = {'date', 'speaker', 'talk title'}
    assert required.issubset(set(header)), header
    idx = {name: header.index(name) for name in required}
    talks = []
    for line in lines[1:]:
        if not line.strip():
            continue
        parts = line.split(',', 2)
        assert len(parts) == 3, line
        talks.append({'date': parts[idx['date']].strip(), 'speaker': parts[idx['speaker']].strip(), 'title': parts[idx['talk title']].strip()})
    assert talks, lines
    return talks

def parse_emails_exact(content):
    seen = set()
    emails = []
    for token in content.replace(',', ' ').split():
        email = token.strip()
        if '@' in email:
            key = email.lower()
            if key not in seen:
                seen.add(key)
                emails.append(email)
    assert emails, content
    return emails

def derive_template_path(paths, basename):
    matches = [p for p in paths if p.split('/')[-1].lower() == basename.lower()]
    assert len(matches) == 1, paths
    return matches[0]

def assert_scheduled_send_at(draft, expected_date, expected_time='09:00:00'):
    expected = datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(expected_date + 'T' + expected_time)
    actual = str(draft.get('scheduled_send_at', '')).replace('|', 'T')
    assert actual, draft
    assert datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(actual) == expected, (draft, expected)
```

def csv_field(s):
    s = str(s)
    if any(c in s for c in [',', chr(34), chr(10), chr(13)]):
        return chr(34) + s.replace(chr(34), chr(34) + chr(34)) + chr(34)
    return s

def paginate_category(api_name, **extra):
    out = []
    page_index = 0
    while True:
        page = guarded_call('gmail', api_name, getattr(apis.gmail, api_name), access_token=gmail_access_token, page_index=page_index, page_limit=20, **extra)
        assert isinstance(page, list), (api_name, page)
        if not page:
            break
        out.extend(page)
        page_index += 1
    return out

def collect_all_threads():
    threads = {}
    for cat in ['show_inbox_threads', 'show_outbox_threads', 'show_archived_threads', 'show_spam_threads']:
        for t in paginate_category(cat):
            threads[t['email_thread_id']] = t
    return threads

def collect_by_label(label):
    found = {}
    for cat in ['show_inbox_threads', 'show_outbox_threads', 'show_archived_threads', 'show_spam_threads']:
        for t in paginate_category(cat, label=label):
            found[t['email_thread_id']] = t
    return found

[code-00448] # Gmail bulk mark-read before last calendar month (requires inspected_apis, inspect_api, ensure_inspected, guarded_call, preflight_apis, get_password, passwords, supervisor_email).
import datetime
def parse_current_date(date_str):
    month_map = {'january':1,'february':2,'march':3,'april':4,'may':5,'june':6,'july':7,'august':8,'september':9,'october':10,'november':11,'december':12}
    parts = [p.strip() for p in date_str.split(',')]
    assert len(parts) == 3, date_str
    month = month_map[parts[1].split()[0].lower()]
    day = int(parts[1].split()[1])
    year = int(parts[2])
    return datetime.date(year, month, day)
def first_of_previous_month(d):
    if d.month == 1:
        return datetime.date(d.year - 1, 12, 1)
    return datetime.date(d.year, d.month - 1, 1)
def thread_date(created_at):
    date_part = str(created_at).split('T')[0].split()[0]
    y, m, d = [int(x) for x in date_part.split('-')]
    return datetime.date(y, m, d)

def norm(s): return ' '.join(str(s or '').lower().replace('-', ' ').split())
amazon_domains = ['amazon.com']
amz_re = re.compile(r'\bAMZ[A-Za-z0-9]+\b', re.IGNORECASE)
arrow_re = re.compile(r'=>\s*([A-Za-z0-9]+)', re.IGNORECASE)
text_exts = ('.txt', '.csv', '.json')
codes = set()
skipped = []
attachment_inspected = []
attachment_limitations = []
fs_access_token = None
def scan_text(text, context):
    if not context:
        return
    for m in amz_re.findall(text):
        codes.add(m.upper())
    for m in arrow_re.findall(text):
        if m.upper().startswith('AMZ'):
            codes.add(m.upper())
for tid, t in threads.items():
    subj = norm(t.get('subject', ''))
    parts = ' '.join(norm(p.get('email', '')) + ' ' + norm(p.get('name', '')) for p in t.get('participants', []))
    thread_context = ('amazon' in subj) or any(d in parts for d in amazon_domains)
    for eid in t.get('email_ids', []):
        try:
            e = guarded_call('gmail','show_email', apis.gmail.show_email, email_id=eid, access_token=gmail_access_token)
        except Exception as ex:
            skipped.append((eid, str(ex)))
            continue
        if not isinstance(e, dict) or 'body' not in e:
            skipped.append((eid, 'no body'))
            continue
        sender_email = str(e.get('sender', {}).get('email', '')).lower()
        body = str(e.get('body', ''))
        body_norm = norm(body)
        email_context = thread_context or any(d in sender_email for d in amazon_domains) or ('amazon' in body_norm) or ('promo code =>' in body_norm)
        scan_text(body, email_context)
        for att in e.get('attachments', []) or []:
            fname = str(att.get('file_name', ''))
            if fname.lower().endswith(text_exts) and email_context:
                if fs_access_token is None:
                    fs_password = get_password(passwords, 'file_system')
                    fs_login = guarded_call('file_system','login', apis.file_system.login, username=supervisor_email, password=fs_password)
                    assert isinstance(fs_login, dict) and 'access_token' in fs_login, fs_login
                    fs_access_token = fs_login['access_token']
                download = guarded_call('gmail','download_attachment', apis.gmail.download_attachment, attachment_id=att['id'], file_system_access_token=fs_access_token, access_token=gmail_access_token, overwrite=True)
                assert isinstance(download, dict) and 'file_path' in download, download
                file_info = guarded_call('file_system','show_file', apis.file_system.show_file, file_path=download['file_path'], access_token=fs_access_token)
                assert isinstance(file_info, dict) and 'content' in file_info, file_info
                scan_text(str(file_info.get('content', '')), email_context)
                attachment_inspected.append((eid, fname))
            elif att:
                attachment_limitations.append((eid, fname, 'binary/non-text or no Amazon/promo context'))
assert codes, (skipped, attachment_limitations)
assert all(c.upper().startswith('AMZ') for c in codes), codes
answer = ', '.join(sorted(codes))
print('codes count:', len(codes), 'skipped:', len(skipped), 'attachments inspected:', len(attachment_inspected), 'attachment limitations:', len(attachment_limitations))
result = guarded_call('supervisor','complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task, answer=answer)
assert isinstance(result, dict) and 'message' in result, result
print(result)
# If no USER output is observed, reissue only a minimal closed guarded_call('supervisor','complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task, answer=answer) block; do not rescan unless answer is missing.
[code-00476] # Self-contained Gmail bulk delete-thread completion recovery (use only after delete_thread and read-back verification succeeded but no USER output from supervisor.complete_task; do not repeat delete_thread or re-query archived threads):
inspected_apis = set()
def inspect_api(app, api):
    doc = apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name=app, api_name=api)
    inspected_apis.add((app, api))
    return doc
def ensure_inspected(app, api):
    assert (app, api) in inspected_apis, (app, api)
def guarded_call(app, api, fn, **kwargs):
    if (app, api) not in inspected_apis:
        inspect_api(app, api)
    ensure_inspected(app, api)
    return fn(**kwargs)
def preflight_apis(app_apis):
    for app, api in app_apis:
        inspect_api(app, api)
        ensure_inspected(app, api)
preflight_apis([('supervisor', 'complete_task')])
result = guarded_call('supervisor', 'complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task)
assert isinstance(result, dict) and 'message' in result, result
print(result)
# End the code block with triple backticks followed by a newline.

[code-00523] Lunch-order email to public Venmo request mechanical template (requires guarded_call, preflight_apis, get_password, norm, parse_current_date, passwords, gmail_access_token, venmo_access_token, yesterday, threads): derive supervisor_email from supervisor.show_account/show_profile when documented; if absent, record that no authoritative supervisor-email API is available and assert the prompt email is non-empty. [code-00535] # Splitwise Amazon color-split expense template (requires guarded_call, norm, parse_current_date, order_date, groups, amazon_access_token, splitwise_access_token).
import datetime
current_date = parse_current_date(guarded_call('phone','get_current_date_and_time', apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time)['date'])
yesterday = current_date - datetime.timedelta(days=1)
orders = []
page_index = 0
while True:
    page = guarded_call('amazon','show_orders', apis.amazon.show_orders, access_token=amazon_access_token, sort_by='-created_at', page_index=page_index, page_limit=20)
    assert isinstance(page, list), page
    if not page: break
    orders.extend(page); page_index += 1
shares_cents = {'glen':0,'andrew':0,'robin':0}
for o in orders:
    if order_date(o['created_at']) != yesterday: continue
    for it in o['order_items']:
        p = guarded_call('amazon','show_product', apis.amazon.show_product, product_id=it['product_id'])
        assert isinstance(p, dict) and 'message' not in p, p
        if norm(p['product_type']) != 't shirt': continue
        cents = int(round(it['ordered_quantity'] * it['price'] * 100))
        color = norm(p['color'])
        if color == 'navy blue': shares_cents['glen'] += cents
        elif color == 'yellow': shares_cents['andrew'] += cents
        else: shares_cents['robin'] += cents
total_cents = sum(shares_cents.values()); assert total_cents > 0
group_matches = [g for g in groups if {'glen','andrew','robin'} <= {norm(m['name']).split()[0] for m in g.get('members', [])}]
assert len(group_matches) == 1, group_matches
email_by_first = {norm(m['name']).split()[0]: m['email'] for m in group_matches[0]['members']}
debtor_emails = [email_by_first['glen'], email_by_first['andrew'], email_by_first['robin']]
debt_amounts = [round(shares_cents[k]/100.0,2) for k in ['glen','andrew','robin']]
paid_amount = round(total_cents/100.0,2)
assert sum(int(round(x*100)) for x in debt_amounts) == total_cents
expense = guarded_call('splitwise','record_expense', apis.splitwise.record_expense, description='T-shirts split by color', paid_amount=paid_amount, payer_email=email_by_first['glen'], debtor_emails=debtor_emails, debt_amounts=debt_amounts, group_id=group_matches[0]['group_id'], access_token=splitwise_access_token)
assert 'expense_id' in expense, expense
read = guarded_call('splitwise','show_expense', apis.splitwise.show_expense, expense_id=expense['expense_id'], access_token=splitwise_access_token)
assert read['group_id'] == group_matches[0]['group_id'] and round(read['paid_amount'],2) == paid_amount
shares_by_email = {s['debtor']['email']: round(s['debt_amount'],2) for s in read['shares']}
assert shares_by_email == dict(zip(debtor_emails, debt_amounts)), shares_by_email
result = guarded_call('supervisor','complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task)
assert isinstance(result, dict) and 'message' in result, result
print(result)
