## COMMON MISTAKES AND CORRECT STRATEGIES

[cms-00010] Do not hardcode product IDs after visually inspecting API output. Derive selections programmatically from collected data, e.g. filter products where seller_id == best_seller_id and inventory_quantity > 0. Also do not rely only on product search to discover sellers when seller APIs exist; use show_seller/search_sellers as the authoritative source for seller ratings.
[cms-00016] Do not hardcode payment_card_id or address_id after visually inspecting API output. Derive them programmatically from show_payment_cards and show_addresses by filtering for the requested card_name and address name, assert exactly one match (or use a deterministic tie-breaker), store selected_payment_card_id and selected_address_id in variables, and pass those variables to place_order.
[cms-00025] Do not choose the lowest payment_card_id when the task does not specify a card. Cards may be expired. Derive selected_payment_card_id from show_payment_cards after filtering for non-expired cards: if current date is known, keep cards with (expiry_year, expiry_month) >= current date; if not, choose the latest expiry as a safe heuristic and assert it is not expired. Store the ID in a variable and pass it to place_order. If place_order fails with an expired-card error, re-derive a valid card programmatically; do not hardcode a replacement ID.
[cms-00027] Do not substitute API-doc inspection for actual verification calls. Before adding selected products to the cart, call apis.amazon.show_product(product_id=pid) for every pid in selected_product_ids and assert the returned product fields (product_id, product_type, seller_id, rating, inventory_quantity, name, description) satisfy the task constraints; search_products output alone is not sufficient ground truth.
[cms-00032] Do not skip API-doc inspection for seemingly simple verification/completion APIs such as amazon.show_product and supervisor.complete_task; call apis.api_docs.show_api_doc before their first use, even if related APIs were already inspected.
[cms-00035] For wishlist purchases, do not manually copy product IDs from printed show_wish_list output into candidate_ids or assign selected_product_id = <literal> after visual inspection. Derive candidate_ids from the wishlist list by filtering product_name/category, then assign selected_product_id = selected['product_id'] after programmatic dimension/inventory/semantic filtering.
[cms-00041] When no payment card is specified, do not pick the first card with max expiry. First confirm no current-date API exists via amazon/supervisor API descriptions, then compute max_expiry = max((expiry_year, expiry_month)), filter cards to that expiry, and apply a deterministic tie-breaker (e.g., min payment_card_id) before assigning selected_payment_card_id; assert exactly one selected card.
[cms-00042] When no shipping address is specified, do not infer Home/Work from product category or item type. Inspect amazon.show_account API doc/response for a default address field; if present, derive selected_address_id from it. If no default address is available, choose a named address only via an explicit deterministic rule and assert exactly one match, recording the justification.
[cms-00045] Do not limit default address/payment-card discovery to amazon.show_account. Also inspect supervisor API descriptions for account/profile APIs that may expose default address or payment-card information. If no default exists, record that fact, then use a deterministic fallback (e.g., max expiry then lowest payment_card_id; lowest address_id) and assert selected_payment_card_id is in show_payment_cards and selected_address_id is in show_addresses before place_order.
[cms-00048] For Amazon review/Q&A evidence tasks, do not hardcode product_id after visually inspecting search_products output. Derive product_id programmatically by filtering search_products results for an exact target name, e.g. matches = [p for p in products if p['name'].lower() == target_name], and assert exactly one match. When the task says to trust only verified purchasers, collect only reviews with is_verified=True and answers with is_verified=True; ignore unverified reviews/answers even if they are more numerous or conflict. If multiple verified statements conflict, apply a deterministic tie-breaker such as most recent created_at, then mode, then lowest source id, and record the rule.
[cms-00052] For Amazon evidence tasks, do not apply purchase/wishlist heuristics (rating/price/inventory/variations/wishlist fit). Lock to one exact normalized product_id: normalize names by lowercasing and replacing hyphens with spaces, assert exactly one match, confirm show_product name/description is the device itself, and reject cases/covers/bundles/accessories. Use only that product_id for reviews/questions/answers unless the task explicitly asks for all variations.
[cms-00053] For attribute evidence, extract only statements with attribute context. For battery hours, require battery/power/charge/charging in the review text or in the question for an answer; parse hours only from that context. Do not scan for any hour mention and do not take the maximum numeric hour in unrelated text.
[cms-00054] Do not use generic call_with_optional_token or try/except TypeError to guess whether access_token is required. Inspect each API doc and pass access_token only when required. After each evidence API call, assert the response has the expected type (list/dict) and does not contain an error/message failure before iterating.
[cms-00060] Do not hardcode the final answer after visually inspecting printed evidence. For evidence tasks, compute the answer into a variable and call supervisor.complete_task(answer=answer) using that variable; if the complete_task schema expects a number and the derived value is whole, pass int(answer), otherwise pass the answer variable directly.
[cms-00061] Treat code-00050's generic parse_hours as unsafe for attribute evidence because it scans any hour mention. For battery-hour or similar attribute tasks, use code-00057 or an equivalent context parser that requires the attribute keyword (e.g., battery/power/charge/charging) before parsing hours, and for Q&A pass both the question and answer so battery-related questions are considered.
[cms-00063] When a task says 'use Discover if it's already in my account, otherwise use what I have in it', do not assume Discover exists or hardcode its payment_card_id. Programmatically filter show_payment_cards by card_name.lower() == 'discover'. If matches exist, select deterministically (e.g., min payment_card_id or assert exactly one); if none, apply the unspecified-card fallback after checking for a current-date API. Always assert selected_payment_card_id is in the cards list before place_order.
[cms-00064] Do not restore cart state by mapping only product_id to quantity. Cart state includes per-item gift_wrap_quantity and cart-level promo_code/promo_valid. Snapshot the full original cart before mutation, then after ordering restore every item with exact quantity, reapply gift wrapping and promo code if present, and assert the restored cart_items and promo fields match the snapshot. If the original promo_code was null, verify the restored cart also has no active promo code.
[cms-00071] Do not select a named address or card with next(...) without materializing matches. For Home/Work/Visa, create matching list, assert len(matches) == 1 or use deterministic tie-breaker; for conditional 'use X if present, otherwise fallback', assert len(matches) == 0 before fallback.
[cms-00072] Before applying an unspecified-card expiry heuristic, inspect and query amazon.show_account and supervisor.show_payment_cards docs/responses for default_payment_card_id; if no default field exists, record that fact and then use authoritative current date or latest-expiry fallback.
[cms-00078] For conditional named payment cards ('use HSBC/Discover/Visa if already in account'), presence alone is not sufficient before place_order. If show_payment_cards exposes expiry_year/expiry_month and phone.get_current_date_and_time is available, assert the selected named card is not expired; if it is expired, treat it as unusable and apply the unspecified-card fallback among valid cards using latest expiry then deterministic tie-breaker.
[cms-00082] Do not hardcode current year/month after visually reading apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time() output. Parse the returned date string programmatically, e.g. current_ym = parse_current_ym(apis.phone.get_current_date_and_time()['date']), before filtering payment cards by expiry.
[cms-00083] For cheapest auxiliary items, do not choose a product_id from printed search results by eye. Filter valid in-stock candidates, sort by price with a deterministic tie-breaker, assign selected_auxiliary_id = cheapest['product_id'], call show_product(selected_auxiliary_id), and use that variable in add_product_to_cart.
[cms-00087] Do not infer absence of default_payment_card_id or default_address_id from API-doc response schemas alone. Before unspecified-card/address fallback, call amazon.show_account and supervisor.show_payment_cards/show_addresses (as applicable), assert the returned account/profile objects lack default fields, and record that absence; only then use current-date/latest-expiry and deterministic address fallback.
[cms-00095] Do not use show_orders(query=...) alone to identify orders from 'this week'; query relevance can return unrelated historical orders. Paginate all orders with sort_by='-created_at', use an authoritative current date, explicitly define the week convention (e.g., Monday-to-current, Sunday-to-current, or rolling 7 days), record it, and filter by created_at >= window_start.
[cms-00099] Do not hardcode final answers, message bodies, or recipient identifiers after visually reading API output. For answer tasks assign answer = selected_object['field'] (e.g., most_liked_song['title']) and call complete_task(answer=answer); for messaging tasks derive recipient_phone_number from search_contacts matches and reply_text from parsed note content, then pass those variables. If a parser returns empty, inspect the raw response schema and fix the parser; never manually type the expected value.
[cms-00100] For contact/messaging tasks, resolve the recipient programmatically: paginate search_contacts if paginated, filter by normalized first/last name or explicit relationship, assert exactly one match (or apply a recorded deterministic tie-breaker), store matches[0]['phone_number'] in recipient_phone_number, and pass that variable to send_text_message. Never copy a phone number from printed contact output.
[cms-00101] Do not assume a single API response is complete for list endpoints outside Amazon evidence. Check docs for page_index/page_limit and paginate until an empty page for phone.search_contacts, phone.search_text_messages, simple_note.search_notes, spotify.show_playlist_library, and similar list APIs; assert each page is a list and not an error dict before extending.
[cms-00107] Do not mix normalized text with raw hyphenated substrings. If norm() lowercases and replaces hyphens with spaces, search only for normalized terms (e.g., 't shirt') or, preferably, use exact normalized equality: norm(product['name']) == norm(target). For Amazon return tasks involving a named product, classify items by exact normalized product name and normalized relative_size, not by keyword fragments that can produce false negatives or false positives.
[cms-00111] Do not use next(...) to select account passwords without asserting uniqueness. For supervisor.show_account_passwords, build matching = [item for item in passwords if item['account_name'] == app_name], assert len(matching) == 1 (or record deterministic tie-breaker), then password = matching[0]['password']; pass that variable to login.
[cms-00112] For apparel category purchases, product_type alone can include adjacent subcategories (e.g., product_type 't-shirt' may include undershirts). Apply explicit semantic include/exclude terms from the task: if 't-shirt' means outer shirt, exclude 'undershirt' unless allowed; otherwise prefer candidates whose normalized name/description match the requested category and record the rule before deterministic selection.
[cms-00118] Do not treat inspect_api as sufficient for API-doc compliance. Every first API call must be immediately preceded in the same code block by ensure_inspected(app, api); docs inspected in earlier blocks do not count. Before executing a block, scan all apis.<app>.<api>( calls and add ensure_inspected for each first use, including amazon.show_orders, show_product, search_products, evidence APIs, show_cart, add_product_to_cart, place_order, show_order, clear_cart, and supervisor.complete_task.
[cms-00120] Do not validate API responses only with isinstance(dict/list). After show_product assert no failure message and required keys product_id, product_type, name, relative_size, inventory_quantity; after show_cart assert cart_items and total_cost; after show_order assert order_id/address_id/payment_card_id/order_items/paid_amount; after evidence pages assert list and no error dict before extending.
[cms-00122] Do not use ensure_inspected as an inspection call. ensure_inspected only asserts that (app, api) is already in inspected_apis; it does not call show_api_doc or add the tuple. If the assertion fails, call inspect_api(app, api) first, then ensure_inspected, then the API. For APIs inspected in an earlier block, still call ensure_inspected immediately before the first actual call in the current block, or use a guarded_call helper that performs this check mechanically.
[cms-00124] Do not compare normalized hyphenated Amazon category values to raw hyphenated strings. norm('t-shirt') becomes 't shirt', so `norm(product_type) == 't-shirt'` is always false. For t-shirt tasks, use raw equality `product_type == 't-shirt'` or normalized equality `norm(product_type) == 't shirt'`; apply the same rule to relative_size and product names.
[cms-00128] Do not sum monetary API fields without validation or rounding: after show_orders, assert paid_amount is present and numeric, deduplicate orders by order_id, and round the final total to 2 decimals before complete_task. Do not rely on visual order counts or include duplicate pagination rows.
[cms-00131] Do not index login_result['access_token'] or date_info['date'] without first asserting the response is a dict and contains the key; malformed responses should fail with a clear assertion, not KeyError. This applies before using credentials or authoritative dates for spend aggregation.
[cms-00133] Do not treat bootstrap api_docs APIs as missing inspected_apis entries. inspected_apis/ensure_inspected/guarded_call enforcement applies to app APIs such as supervisor.show_account_passwords, amazon.login, phone.get_current_date_and_time, amazon.show_orders, and supervisor.complete_task. Do not call apis.api_docs.show_api_doc on api_docs.show_app_descriptions, api_docs.show_api_descriptions, or api_docs.show_api_doc; use them directly as documentation bootstrap.
[cms-00136] Do not treat gmail.show_inbox_threads as a single-page list or hardcode email_id/attachment_id from printed output. Paginate inbox threads when documented, select the relevant thread by normalized subject/sender, derive email_id and attachment_id programmatically, then download and read the attachment. Do not parse checklist items by eye; convert file content into requested_specs = [(name, quantity), ...] with assertions, and use those variables for Amazon product resolution.
[cms-00144] Do not end a task after only inspecting supervisor.complete_task or emitting a final code block without observed success. The final block must execute guarded_call('supervisor', 'complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task) (or with answer), assert the returned dict contains 'message', print it, and end with ``` followed by a newline. If no execution output is shown, reissue the completion call.
[cms-00149] Do not let the presence of an authoritative current-date API bypass default payment-card discovery. For unspecified-card purchases, first inspect/call supervisor.show_payment_cards (and amazon.show_account) to check for default_payment_card_id; if no default field is present, record that absence, then use phone.get_current_date_and_time to filter non-expired Amazon cards and choose latest expiry with a deterministic tie-breaker.
[cms-00153] Do not accept adjacent product_type for an explicitly requested category merely because name/description contains the category term. For 'office chair', require product_type == 'office chair' unless the task explicitly allows adjacent categories; exclude gaming chair, dining chair, etc. Build candidates programmatically, verify with show_product, then assign selected_product_id = selected['product_id'] using deterministic key (e.g., highest rating, then lower price, then lower product_id). Never copy a printed product_id into later code.
[cms-00154] Do not rely on guarded_call's lazy inspection to satisfy pre-block inspection for conditional cart-restoration APIs. Before executing any block containing conditional calls such as add_gift_wrapping_to_product or apply_promo_code_to_cart, explicitly inspect_api for every API referenced in those branches, even if the branch is false; guarded_call may inspect at call time, but the hard rule requires inspection before block execution.
[cms-00159] Do not pass API-call kwargs into inspect_api. inspect_api(app, api) only accepts the app and api names; calling inspect_api(amazon, add_product_to_cart, product_id=..., access_token=...) raises TypeError and can leave the API unregistered, causing ensure_inspected to fail. Use guarded_call(app, api, fn, **kwargs), or inspect_api(app, api); ensure_inspected(app, api); then call the API.
[cms-00160] Do not decide an Amazon address is missing by visually comparing printed show_addresses output. Build target_addr from the sender contact record, programmatically filter amazon.show_addresses with normalized street/city/state/country/zip equality, and call add_address only if matches is empty; after adding, re-fetch show_addresses and derive selected_address_id from the programmatic match.
[cms-00163] Do not place inspect_api calls for conditional APIs inside the conditional branch. If the branch is false, the API is never inspected. At the top of every code block, pre-inspect every app API referenced anywhere in the block, including false branches and restoration APIs such as add_gift_wrapping_to_product and apply_promo_code_to_cart; then call ensure_inspected immediately before each first call in that same block.
[cms-00169] Do not hardcode relevant_thread_ids or checklist_email_id after visually reading Gmail output. Build them from normalized subject/body/participants and assert uniqueness; if multiple candidate threads exist, read emails and keep the one whose body contains checklist markers and whose replies are response_to_email_id. Do not treat email participants as proof of sibling/family relationships.
[cms-00175] Do not select checklist items using only search_products(product_type=item) and exact product_type equality. Amazon category labels may differ from checklist wording. For each remaining item, union paginated search_products(product_type=item) and search_products(query=item), deduplicate by product_id, verify candidates with show_product, accept if product_type matches or normalized name/description semantically contains the item, filter inventory_quantity >= requested_quantity, then choose highest product rating with deterministic tie-breakers.
[cms-00180] Do not emit bare apis.amazon.clear_cart in a cart-restoration block even if clear_cart was inspected earlier. Use guarded_call('amazon', 'clear_cart', apis.amazon.clear_cart, access_token=amazon_access_token) or inspect_api/ensure_inspected immediately before the first call in that same block; the same applies to add_product_to_cart, show_cart, gift-wrap, and promo APIs during restoration.
[cms-00181] Do not interpret 'verify selected products' as verifying every Amazon search candidate. For checklist item selection with many candidates, verify only the deterministic top candidate(s) with show_product; if a candidate fails inventory/type/name checks, move to the next top-K candidate. Never respond to a request-limit error by hardcoding a product_id from printed output.
[cms-00185] Do not select verified[0] as the chosen product unless the verified list has been explicitly sorted by a deterministic key. After filtering candidates, sort by a recorded key (e.g., rating descending, price ascending, product_id ascending) and assign selected_product_id = sorted_candidates[0]['product_id']; assert the selected item satisfies all constraints. This prevents arbitrary selection when multiple qualifying products exist.
[cms-00190] Do not resolve a singular relationship such as 'husband' by substring matching normalized relationships; 'ex-husband' or similar labels can match. Use exact normalized equality (norm(rel) == 'husband') or the documented relationship filter, and assert exactly one contact with a non-empty phone_number. If substring matching is unavoidable, explicitly exclude ex-husband/ex-spouse/ex-partner.
[cms-00195] For irreversible messaging tasks, do not resend a text message when only completion is unproven. If `phone.send_text_message` has returned a success dict, treat the send as done; if `supervisor.complete_task` was emitted but no observed USER output is available, reissue only a minimal final block that calls `guarded_call('supervisor', 'complete_task', apis.supervisor.complete_task)` (no answer for action tasks), asserts the result dict contains `message`, and prints it. Do not re-fetch the wishlist, rebuild the message, or re-run send_text_message unless required variables are missing.
[cms-00201] Do not restore cart state with set equality or only product_id->quantity mapping. Use sorted lists of (product_id, quantity, gift_wrap_quantity) and assert len(restored['cart_items']) == len(orig_items). If the original cart had no gift wrap, assert every restored item has gift_wrap_quantity == 0; if the original promo_code was None/empty, assert restored promo_code is None/empty and promo_valid is false/None.
[cms-00211] Do not decide the block-local preflight list from runtime data. Even if gift_wrap_quantity == 0 and promo_code is None, any guarded_call('amazon','add_gift_wrapping_to_product',...) or guarded_call('amazon','apply_promo_code_to_cart',...) appearing in the block source must be inspected before execution; false branches never call guarded_call.
[cms-00215] Do not fulfill 'deliver one to parents' home and one to my home' with a single quantity-2 order or by assuming per-item addresses/wrapping in one order. Derive parents_address_id from relationship contacts and my_home_address_id from exact normalized Amazon address name 'home', assert the IDs are distinct, then place two orders and assert each order's address_id and gift_wrap_quantity.
[cms-00223] Do not resolve 'mother's home' (or 'father's home') by accepting generic 'parent' or substring matches. Match exact normalized relationship labels such as 'mother' (or documented synonym 'mom') and use that contact's home_address; if multiple contacts match, assert identical home_address values or apply a recorded deterministic rule. For 'my home', use the saved Amazon address named 'Home' rather than the relationship contact's address.
[cms-00228] Do not apply the 'all relationship home addresses must be identical' rule to tasks that require separate deliveries to each audience member. For 'each sibling's home', treat differing home addresses as required separate orders; only use the identical-address consistency rule when the task names a single shared location (e.g., parents' home).
[cms-00230] Do not resolve plural relationship audiences such as 'brothers' or 'sisters' by substring matching normalized relationships; 'brother-in-law', 'step-brother', or similar labels may be included. Use exact normalized relationship labels ('brother', 'sister') unless the task explicitly includes in-law/step, or apply explicit exclusions. Assert the resulting audience is non-empty and deduplicate by contact_id.
[cms-00238] Do not add a literal post-selection assertion such as assert selected_product_id == 144 after programmatically selecting a product. This hardcodes an observed ID and can fail if the environment changes. [cms-00243] Do not treat a color_available boolean as sufficient for color-specific purchases. [cms-00252] Do not manually copy product names or IDs from printed phone messages, and do not treat a user task category such as 'food scale' as optional when the message offers two alternatives. [cms-00259] Do not assume a single relationship contact identifies the requester. [cms-00276] Do not wrap APIs in a generic call_api that filters kwargs by parsed doc parameter names or catches broad exceptions to keep going. [cms-00283] Do not conflate 'include available items' with 'all requested items must be available'. ## PROBLEM-SOLVING HEURISTICS AND WORKFLOWS
[psw-00002] Remember you can use the variables in your code in subsequent code blocks.
[psw-00007] Many APIs return items in "pages". Make sure to run through all the pages by looping over `page_index`.

## VERIFICATION CHECKLIST

[vc-00012] Before irreversible purchase actions, verify: (1) selected product via show_product(product_id=...) has expected product_type and seller_id; (2) payment card via show_payment_cards has card_name matching requested card; (3) address via show_addresses has name matching requested address; (4) cart via show_cart contains only intended product; (5) after place_order, show_order(order_id=...) confirms address_id, payment_card_id, and ordered product. Check API docs for show_cart and show_order even if they seem common.
[vc-00018] Before irreversible purchase actions, add defensive assertions: winning-seller candidate list is non-empty; selected product has expected product_type, seller_id, and inventory_quantity > 0; API responses do not contain failure messages; payment card and address filters return exactly one requested match; cart contains only selected_product_id; final order uses selected_product_id, selected_payment_card_id, and selected_address_id variables.
[vc-00021] For marketplace category purchases, do not trust product_type alone because listings can be mislabeled. Before irreversible purchase, filter/assert that the selected product's name/description semantically match the requested category and exclude obvious mismatches (e.g., for 'portable air conditioner', require 'air conditioner' and reject 'tv'/'television'). Apply this semantic filter before product rating/price tie-breakers.
[vc-00022] Assert exact cart and order quantities: after add_product_to_cart, show_cart must have len(cart['cart_items']) == 1, item['product_id'] == selected_product_id, and item['quantity'] == 1; after place_order, show_order must have len(order['order_items']) == 1, item['product_id'] == selected_product_id, and item['ordered_quantity'] == 1.
[vc-00026] For multi-item purchases, derive selected_product_ids from the qualifying product list and use that list for show_product, add_product_to_cart, cart, and order verification. If the task says items need not be identical, prefer N distinct qualifying products with quantity 1 each when available. Add the first product with clear_cart_first=True and subsequent products with clear_cart_first=False. Assert cart has len(cart_items)==N, product_id set equals selected_product_ids, and each quantity==1; assert order has len(order_items)==N and each ordered_quantity==1.
[vc-00028] For category-count purchases, apply the strictest requested category term first (e.g., require 'laundry hamper' for laundry hampers) and reject adjacent categories (e.g., 'laundry sorter') unless show_product description explicitly identifies the item as a hamper. If fewer than N strict matches remain after authoritative seller/product rating and inventory checks, broaden with safe related query terms (e.g., laundry basket, hamper), reapply product_type and semantic validation, and only then consider borderline items; never loosen semantics merely to reach N.
[vc-00029] Assert success for cart/order mutations instead of only printing responses: after each add_product_to_cart, require a success message such as 'added to the cart' and no failure message; after place_order, require order_id and a success message before show_order. Then verify show_order address_id, payment_card_id, product_id set, and ordered_quantity exactly match the selected values.
[vc-00033] For category-count purchases with rating thresholds, build a complete candidate set by unioning paginated search_products(product_type=...) and search_products(query=...) results, deduplicate by product_id, apply min_product_rating/min_seller_rating filters, then filter by authoritative seller rating, product rating, inventory, and semantic match before selecting N distinct products; if fewer than N remain, enumerate candidate sellers via search_sellers/show_product_feature_choices and search by seller_id before broadening semantics.
[vc-00037] For physical-fit purchases, assert selected product programmatically: inventory_quantity > 0; length/width satisfy countertop limits with <= (allow equality; use epsilon only for floating-point comparisons); if orientation is not fixed, accept swapped dimensions. If variations can affect size, inspect each variation's show_product dimensions and filter; if variations are not allowed, restrict to wishlist product_ids. Choose deterministically among fitting products (e.g., lowest price, then highest rating, then lowest product_id).
[vc-00040] For wishlist physical-fit purchases, before irreversible actions assert: selected_product_id is in the original show_wish_list product_ids; show_product(product_id=selected_product_id)['product_type'] matches the requested category (e.g., 'cutting board'); inventory_quantity > 0; and authoritative length/width fit the constraint with swapped orientation allowed. Do not rely only on product_name matching.
[vc-00049] Before answering an Amazon evidence task, assert: (1) product_id was derived programmatically from search_products by exact name match; (2) optional show_product(product_id=...) confirms the selected product name/type; (3) all review and answer pages were fetched with while True pagination; (4) every extracted statement came from a source with is_verified=True; (5) no unverified conflicting statement was used; (6) supervisor.complete_task API doc was inspected and the answer type matches the schema. For questions phrased as 'How many hours', prefer returning the numeric value, e.g. 4, unless the complete_task schema or task explicitly requires a string with units.
[vc-00055] Before answering an Amazon attribute-evidence task, assert: (1) one exact normalized product_id was selected and no variation product_ids were used; (2) all reviews/answers are is_verified=True; (3) every extracted value has attribute context or comes from an attribute-related question; (4) the final answer uses one explicit deterministic rule (e.g., latest created_at then lowest source_id; use mode only if timestamps are missing) and not a tautological mode/latest combination; (5) complete_task schema was inspected and numeric answer type matches it.
[vc-00059] For Amazon evidence/search pagination, after every call to search_products, show_product_reviews, show_product_questions, or show_product_question_answers, assert that the page is a list and not an error dict (e.g., {'message': ...}); if the response is a dict or contains a failure message, stop and diagnose instead of extending the collected evidence.
[vc-00065] For multi-item orders with explicitly named products, derive selected_product_ids from separate exact-match variables and assert exactly one normalized match per requested product (e.g., assert len(ps5_matches) == 1; selected_product_ids = [ps5_matches[0]['product_id'], scale_matches[0]['product_id'], xbox_matches[0]['product_id']]). Never replace these with literals copied from printed output. Before place_order, show_product each selected id, assert normalized name and inventory, and assert cart product_id set equals selected_product_ids with quantity 1; after show_order, assert order product_id set and ordered_quantity match the same variables.
[vc-00073] For multi-quantity named-product orders, build requested_items = [(product_id, requested_quantity), ...]; assert show_product(product_id)['inventory_quantity'] >= requested_quantity for each; add exact quantity; assert cart and show_order have identical (product_id, quantity/ordered_quantity) sets.
[vc-00074] After place_order, verify financial state: compare pre-order show_cart total_cost or expected sum(price * ordered_quantity + tax + delivery_fee + gift_wrap_fee - discount) with show_order paid_amount; assert address_id, payment_card_id, and order_items match selected values before restoring cart.
[vc-00075] Before restoring a snapshot cart, call show_product for each original cart item and assert inventory_quantity >= original quantity; if an item is unavailable, diagnose before continuing.
[vc-00084] For gift-wrap cost-comparison orders, before place_order assert: Option A cart has the main item gift_wrap_quantity equal to the intended quantity and option_a_total saved; Option B cart has the main item gift_wrap_quantity == 0 and the selected auxiliary wrapper present with option_b_total saved; assert chosen_total < other_total or apply a recorded deterministic tie-breaker; after place_order assert paid_amount equals chosen_total and order_items product_id set equals the selected main/auxiliary product_id variables.
[vc-00097] Before irreversible initiate_return, assert: (1) the date window convention is explicit and orders were filtered by created_at, not query relevance; (2) return_items/keep_items were built programmatically from show_orders and show_product; (3) quantity_to_return = ordered_quantity - returned_quantity > 0; (4) the keep-size product_id is absent from return_items; (5) deliverer_id was selected from show_return_deliverers. ## TROUBLESHOOTING AND PITFALLS:

[ts-00011] If a paginated API such as amazon.search_product_types fails with AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'cursor', diagnose pagination behavior before abandoning it: retry with default page_limit or omit page_limit, or fall back to a more authoritative API such as search_products(product_type=...). Do not assume the failed API is unusable without trying a safer pagination pattern.
[ts-00092] If show_cart after place_order returns cart_items=[] but total_cost/delivery_fee is nonzero, treat it as a post-order cart display artifact. Do not try to zero the total by adding/removing items or diagnose it as an unplaced order. Assert cart_items is empty, then restore the pre-order snapshot; if the snapshot was empty, clear the cart and verify cart_items remains empty.
[ts-00115] If clean_candidates is empty, do not choose a dirty candidate or hardcode a product_id. Diagnose the evidence parser first: ensure all pages were fetched, negated statements are ignored, question-only mentions are not counted, and affirmative answers to fade questions are counted; only then broaden candidate search with task-safe related terms.
[ts-00171] If category search returns adjacent items because query substring matches compound words (e.g., backpack matches backpacking tent), do not rely on broad bad_terms that can remove valid items; gate on exact product_type equality and verify show_product.
[ts-00182] If Amazon product selection fails with 'Number of requests exceeded the limit' while looping show_product over candidates, diagnose the candidate universe and switch to metadata-first filtering/top-K verification. Do not reduce verification by manually choosing a printed product_id; re-derive selected product_id programmatically from ranked search candidates and verify only the final selected/top-K products.
[ts-00186] If a note/email/file parser returns an empty list, do not manually copy expected items from truncated printed output. Print repr of the raw content, inspect line prefixes/indentation/bullet markers, fix the parser to extract all matching records, build the message/answer variable programmatically, and assert the parsed list is non-empty before sending or completing.
[ts-00198] If a note/email/file line parser returns empty, check whether you stripped leading whitespace before testing prefixes like ' - director:'. Stripping first can turn ' - director:' into '- director:' and make startswith(' - ') fail. Preserve the original line for prefix checks or use regex, then strip only the extracted value; never hardcode expected items after a failed parser.
[ts-00333] If gmail.show_inbox_threads subject candidates include both a general trip thread and an expense thread, do not choose by printed ID or first candidate. Read all candidate threads with gmail.show_thread and keep only threads whose non-supervisor replies contain all required expense labels; if none contain them, diagnose rather than writing empty rows.
[ts-00341] If gmail.download_attachment returns 422 because download_to_file_path is supposed to be a file and must have an extension, do not retry with another directory. Reissue the download with download_to_file_path omitted (default ~/downloads) or with the exact attachment file_name appended to ~/downloads, preserving extension; then verify file_path.
[ts-00354] If a task-specified directory such as ~/documents/work/recruiting returns 422, do not invent file paths or stop. Call file_system.show_directory on parent paths such as ~/documents or ~/documents/work, or use directory_exists/file_exists, then use the discovered absolute paths for templates, decision files, and generated attachments.
[ts-00388] If a schedule CSV parser returns empty or wrong columns, print repr of raw content and switch to csv.reader; do not manually copy rows. If template file is not found at a guessed path, call file_system.show_directory with substring and entry_type='files', recursive=True, and assert exactly one basename match; do not hardcode absolute path. If gmail.show_draft scheduled_send_at appears as ISO T while create_draft used pipe, normalize pipe to T or parse datetime before equality.
[ts-00392] If `csv.reader` fails with 'Usage of the following function is not allowed', do not retry the csv module or manually copy rows. Switch to a manual parser using `split(',', maxsplit)` when the first fields are comma-free, or implement a quote-aware parser if fields may contain commas; print raw line repr to diagnose.
## OTHERS
[misc-00003] Remember that the email addresses, access tokens and variables (e.g. spotify_password) in the example above are not valid anymore.
[misc-00008] Once you have completed the task, make sure to call apis.supervisor.complete_task(). If the task asked for some information, return it as the answer argument, i.e. call apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=<answer>). Many tasks do not require an answer, so in those cases, just call apis.supervisor.complete_task() i.e. do not pass any argument.
[misc-00009] For marketplace purchase tasks, interpret 'from its highest-rated seller' as the seller with the highest seller rating among sellers that offer the requested product category, not the highest-rated product. Collect all products in the category, extract unique seller_ids, fetch seller ratings with show_seller (or paginate search_sellers and filter to sellers that actually sell the category), choose the maximum rating, then programmatically select an in-stock product from that seller. If the task does not specify product quality, any in-stock product from the winning seller satisfies the seller constraint; use a deterministic tie-breaker such as highest product rating or lowest price.
[misc-00114] For evidence-constrained purchases, use this order: derive size from past orders -> search in-size category candidates -> verify each with show_product -> collect all evidence -> compute clean_candidates -> deterministic select -> snapshot cart/card/address -> order -> verify using variables -> restore cart.
[misc-00139] For tasks where instructions come from a partner/friend/coworker email, treat the email attachment/body as the authoritative checklist; optionally verify the sender relationship via phone.search_contacts when identity matters, but do not block if the email content is clear. If the task mentions an event window such as 'next week', use phone.get_current_date_and_time and product delivery_days only when arrival timing is operationally required; if no exact event date is given, record a conservative assumption and do not reject items unless the task explicitly requires delivery by a date.
